# Agent-harness audit — subagent activity logs

Export date: 2026-09-18. Repository: `/Users/tony/Documents/Biskiq/spatial-sketch-editor`.

## Scope and fidelity

All 12 probes were launched with `fork_turns="none"`. This export contains their task assignments, public commentary, every recorded tool call and tool result, final reports, and Probe I follow-ups. Events are chronological within each probe; probes are grouped A–L.

Private reasoning, internal system/developer instructions, encrypted internal payloads, and duplicated environment/bootstrap envelopes are excluded. Task assignments and follow-up text are restored from the visible parent conversation because their local log payloads are encrypted; other exported activity comes directly from the recorded subagent transcripts. Shared repository instructions were supplied to the agents but are not repeated as a startup envelope. Repository documentation returned by tools is retained in full as recorded.

Tool results retain any original truncation; this export does not recover text absent from the recorded result. Embedded JSON tool wrappers are preserved. No additional summarization or output truncation is applied. G–J used read-only synthetic fixtures; their proposed changes are not executed changes.

This is a **context harvest** — not an implementation plan, not a\nvisual-direction proposal, and not a slice brief.\n**Compiled:** 2026-09-17 against `main` @ `368a799` (P23.13 merged via PR #58; `main`\nHEAD at compile time).\n**Companion baseline:** [`p23-design-context.md`](../../context/p23-design-context.md) — the\nP23-wide product/Plan baseline. Where they disagree, this file wins and the\ndelta is named in §11.\n\nEvery claim is tagged:\n\n| Tag | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| **[D]** | **Durable shell contract** — expected to survive P24/P26 substantially unchanged in role |\n| **[P24]** | **P24-sensitive** — current content/workflow likely to change under Scene staging evolution |\n| **[P26]** | **P26-sensitive** — current assumption likely to evolve as spatial/view architecture expands |\n| **[P23.15]** | **P23.15-sensitive** — current *architectural 3D wall/junction mesh appearance* is evidence, not the visual baseline; the next P23 slice owns derived 3D geometry |\n| **[V]** | **Presentation-only** — pure visual treatment P23.14 may freely overhaul |\n| **[?]** | Unresolved or thin evidence — flagged, not asserted |\n\n**How to read this file:** §5 is the map; §6–§8 are what exists today; §9–§10 are the\nfence and the field; §11–§13 are the load-bearing temporal context (P23.13 settled, then\nP23.15/P24/P26 press); §14–§17 are the working set for concept work.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Purpose\n\nP23.14 is the shell and visual-system slice. It exists because P23.0–P23.13 finished\nthe *substance* of the architectural Build phase — wall-first topology, canonical\ncurves, direct editing, display identity, the Plan drafting visual language — while\nthe shell around that substance settled only far enough to be functional.\n\n**The outcome P23.14 is now accountable for (owner review, 2026-09-17):** establish a\ncoherent, extensible editor-shell **visual system and interaction grammar** with a high\nenough ceiling that future P24, P26 and later capability can normally enter through the\n*same language* without requiring a new shell redesign:\n\n> a durable shell and visual system that (a) reads as one product across Scene/Camera and\n> Plan/3D, (b) can host **P24** staging workflows and **P26** spatial/orthographic view\n> concepts *without a shell rewrite or a second visual language*, and (c) does not freeze\n> today's temporary P24/P26-sensitive content into permanent information architecture.\n\n**P23.14 owns the visual operating system, not speculative future functionality.** It may\nsubstantially revise: shell presentation; hierarchy; layout composition; control grouping;\ndensity; typography; iconography; surfaces; borders and elevation; visual state treatment;\npanel composition; timeline integration; and the canvas/chrome relationship. It also\nowes the reusable grammar other surfaces inherit — property sections and rows, toolbar\ngroups, segmented selectors, mode/tool treatment, panel headers, status/warning/refusal\ntreatments, contextual instrument framing, selection/focus/hover/disabled states,\nhierarchy rows, menu/popover grammar, Timeline shell grammar, and the spacing/type/icon/\ntoken systems. Architecture and ownership contracts are preserved (§9). **[D]** in scope,\n**[V]** in treatment.\n\n**P23.14 does not implement** P24 multi-selection capability, material/light/environment\nworkflows or placement/support semantics; P26 Section, Wall Elevation, Ceiling Focus or\ncrop/depth/reveal behavior; P23.15 junction geometry; or speculative future view controls.\nThose capabilities must have a *clear extension path* through the selected visual system —\nwhich is not the same as shipping an implementation of them (§14).\n\n**What this document is not.** It does not propose a visual direction, pick a style,\nprescribe CSS or a component library, or optimize around current Svelte component\nboundaries. Current styling is explicitly **not** authoritative (§10).\n\n**Declared slice scope** (roadmap reconciliation, 2026-09-14; re-scoped by owner review\n2026-09-17): P23.14 is registered as *\"Editor Shell & Visual System Foundation\"* —\nformerly *\"Build shell, Navigator and Inspector finish\"* with a bounded polish scope. That\nbounded work is **retained inside** the larger outcome rather than dropped, and it still\nabsorbs six open issues: **#34** status-bar hint contrast, **#35** canonical axis tokens in\nnumber fields, **#38** shared context-menu keyboard navigation, **#39** editor tablist\nkeyboard behaviour, **#40** Project Row popover coordination, **#41** Document menu focus\nlifecycle. It also owns the P23.6e Navigator-density follow-up. Source:\n`docs/roadmap/p23-layout-depth/2026-09-14-P23-remaining-roadmap-reconciliation.md` §P23.14. **[D]**\n\n**Concurrent P23 slice, already merged (not P23.14's scope, but it lands beside it).** The\nJunction-dissolve / Wall-join slice — **merged to `main` through PR #57** (`2716f61`,\nimplementation `e43ecf4`, 2026-09-17) and consuming no tracker P-number, per the child-plan\nexception — replaces a degree-2 Junction with one joined Wall. Its shipped entry point is\n**Delete/Backspace on a selected degree-2 Junction in the Plan**, with the transaction\nreporting success or a per-code failure in the status message. Its *optional* entry points\nwere explicitly deferred to P23.14's shell finish: an **Inspector** action, a\nNavigator/tree row action, and a Plan context-menu item. So P23.14's Inspector surface must\nbe able to host a **reason-coded destructive action** (`Delete junction` / join walls)\nbeside property sections, and must be able to show a refusal reason rather than a silent\nno-op. None of those follow-ups may become a second eligibility authority — the core\nplanner owns every eligibility decision. **[D]**\n\n---\n\n## 2. Product mental model\n\n### What Museum Editor is **[D]**\n\nA browser tool for creators who want to author an interactive 3D museum, exhibition or\nwalkthrough — architectural space, placed objects, camera navigation — and publish it as\na visitor-facing web experience. It is explicitly **not** a BIM system, a DCC\nreplacement, Figma/Canva, or a game engine (`docs/reference/north-star.md`).\n\nAuthoring is phase-based, not a waterfall: **Build** (spatial/architectural) → **Stage**\n(objects, materials, lighting) → **Direct** (camera and route) → **Experience** (visitor\njourney). P23 shipped Build; P24 is Stage; P26 deepens Build's spatial/vertical depth;\nP25 is Experience. Phase names are authoring vocabulary, not UI labels. **[D]**\n\n### Who it is for **[D]**\n\nAn individual creator (architect, exhibition designer, museum technologist, or an\nartist building a portfolio space) working alone in a browser, who needs *credible\narchitectural authoring* and *credible camera direction* without CAD or DCC training.\nProduct authorization is single-user project ownership today. No teams, permissions or\ncollaboration exist.\n\n### Core authoring workflows **[D]**\n\n```text\nScene → Plan → Layout    draw Walls / Rooms / Door / Window / Column / Platform /\n                         Plinth, select, reshape, inspect, refine\nScene → Plan → Arrange   place and move Scene objects (own pipeline)\nScene → 3D               verticals, materials, lights, object placement in space\nCamera → Plan / 3D       authored camera nodes, connections, paths, framing\nSave / Preview / Publish  (Row 1 + Publish surface)\n```\n\nUndo/Redo is **one** chronological stack for Spatial, tagged per document (Scene vs\nLayout), exposed in Row 1. **[D]**\n\n### Scene vs Camera **[D]**\n\n`Scene | Camera` is the **domain** axis. **Scene** is the physical space (Layout\narchitecture + placed Scene content). **Camera** is guided cinematic camera navigation\n— \"Camera\" always means a cinematic `PerspectiveCamera` route, never a webcam. Camera\nowns the timeline and the node/connection graph. A domain switch is **attention-only**:\nit never changes the document, history or the world, and never snaps the view.\n\n### Plan vs 3D **[D]** with a P26 caveat **[P26]**\n\n`Plan | 3D` is the **view** axis, orthogonal to domain. All four cells exist and are\nreachable today:\n\n| | Plan | 3D |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Scene** | the Build surface (`Layout` mode) and `Arrange` | spatial 3D of layout + scene |\n| **Camera** | camera-graph authoring over a read-only architectural backdrop | camera authoring in 3D |\n\nBoot is **Scene → Plan**. Camera → Plan is *not* a second layout editor: it authors\ncamera nodes over read-only architecture, with its own toolbar and Inspector.\n\n**The P26 caveat is load-bearing for shell design:** Plan/3D is durable as a *top-level\ndistinction*, but P26 introduces **contextual orthographic instruments** (Section, Wall\nElevation, Ceiling Focus/reflected plan) that are explicitly *not* peer workspaces.\nP26 lists the presentation question — `Plan|3D + Open Section` vs `Plan|Ortho|3D` vs a\nbreadcrumb takeover — as an **open design problem for external proposal**. A shell that\nhard-codes exactly two view slots will need surgery in P26. See §13.\n\n### Scene Plan local modes **[D]**\n\nScene → Plan carries a workspace-local mode presented as `Layout | Arrange` (internal\nvalue is still `staging`; the user-facing word is **Arrange**). This is a *mode inside a\nview inside a domain*, one level below the two global axes. **[?]** How a third level of\nlocality should be signed visually is an open design question (§17).\n\n### Camera Timeline ownership **[D]**\n\nThe Timeline mounts in the **Camera domain only** and is Camera-owned in both Plan and\n3D. It is collapsed `48px` / expanded `288px`, user range `240–300px`, with expansion\nstate persisting verbatim and **never** auto-expanding on a domain switch. Lanes:\nCamera Path, Shots, FOV, Look At, Roll. Collapsed chrome is an integrated 48px temporal\nmini-player (not a second playback bar). Expanded chrome has a fixed 36px header.\n\n**The lane set is not static, and it can be absent entirely.** Lanes resolve from preview\nscope: with **no camera flow** there is no *sequence*, so the dock keeps its header (or its\n48px transport strip when collapsed) and renders an **empty body** — no lane surface, no\nruler — while the scope chip reads `No sequence yet`. The surface is still there; only its\nlane body is empty.\nA single placed camera node does not create a flow, so it produces no lanes either.\nSelecting a **connection** gives the five *edge-local* lanes; a real flow (≥2 connected\nnodes) gives the full sequence lane set. §3 and §7 carry the measured detail, and **no\nscreenshot in §4 shows a populated lane set** — the lane surface is currently specified in\nwords only. **[D]** for scope-dependence; **[V]** for lane presentation.\n\n### How the surfaces relate **[D]**\n\n```text\nviewport      primary work surface — one shared Plan|3D axis, never unmounted, only\n              collapse/clip; direct-manipulation fixtures live here\nrow 1         project identity, persistence, project modes, undo/redo, preview,\n              theme, account — global scope\nrow 2         domain · view · mode · contextual tools — the authoring grammar\nleft rail     Selection/outline surface (Hierarchy|Assets in Scene; the Camera sidebar\n              in Camera) — \"what exists\" and \"what is selected\"\nInspector     owned property surface for the *current selection context* — \"what this is\"\ntimeline      Camera-owned temporal surface, mounted in Camera only\nstatus bar    persistent, informational: cell, selection, save state, hints, grid/snap\noverlays      transient: previews/ghosts, snap markers, invalid refusals, focus ring\n```\n\n**There is one deterministic selection authority**, and viewport, left rail and Inspector\nare *consumers* of it — not three independent selections, and **not** \"exactly one selected\nentity\" as a permanent contract. Current Layout editing may remain single-target; future\nScene staging (P24) may hold an **ordered selection set with one primary/active entity**\nthat every surface resolves identically. Marquee and layout multi-select do **not** exist\ntoday (deferred, issue #28), and the shell must not imply them yet. **[D]** as one\nauthority; **[P24]** for set/primary semantics.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Design to 240–300 px regardless. **[D]**\n\n### Surface-by-surface\n\n#### Row 1 — Project Row `[D]`\n- **Purpose:** project scope and everything global. `‹ Projects` (back to Project Hub),\n  an inline-editable project-name field, a location/session chip (`Local Session` for a\n  guest, cloud state when authenticated), a save-state control (`Save to Cloud`, whose\n  disabled reason explains any blocker), a Document menu (`⋯`) holding per-document\n  JSON import/paste/copy/download/reset plus Layout status and invalid-import feedback,\n  the `Spatial | Publish` project-mode pair, Undo/Redo, Visitor `Preview`, a theme menu,\n  and account/sign-in.\n- **Must stay coherent with:** the save-state control aggregates **scene and layout\n  dirtiness**; Undo/Redo binds the one tagged Spatial history stack; Document-menu focus\n  lifecycle and popover coordination are open P23.14 issues (#40, #41).\n- **Content durability:** **[D]** in role; **[P24]** in that Staging adds more\n  document-level operations, and **[P26]** in that non-Spatial surfaces must *defer* the\n  undo slot rather than share a universal cross-workspace undo.\n- **Current tension:** `Spatial | Publish` reads as a 2-tab mode switch, but Publish is a\n  project-level supporting **surface** (and `Experience` is a third, future, primary\n  mode — P25). Flat 2-tab treatment will not scale. Flagged, not prescribed.\n\n#### Row 2 — Workspace Ribbon `[D]` in role, **[P24]/[P26]** in content\nRight side is stable across all four cells: `Snap` (value dropdown, e.g. `0.25 m`),\n`Grid` toggle, and a panel-visibility group (`collapse left rail`, `collapse right\nrail`, `Focus` — collapse both, `\\`). Centre-left is **entirely cell-dependent**:\n\n| Cell | Mode/scope segment | Tool group |\n|---|---|---|\n| Scene · Plan | `Layout \\| Arrange` | Layout: Select, Wall, Rect Room, Poly Room, Door, Window, Column, Platform, Plinth + `Tour` viewport option |\n| Scene · 3D | — | Select, Move, Rotate, Scale, Add Asset, `Local \\| World` |\n| Camera · Plan | — | Select, Add Camera, Connect, View |\n| Camera · 3D | — | Select, Move, Rotate, Add camera, Path, Frame, View, `Observer \\| POV` |\n\n- **Purpose:** the authoring grammar — domain, view, mode, active tool, and the view's\n  authoring actions.\n- **Must stay coherent with:** the pre-P21 note that contextual/floating toolbars\n  *relocate into Row 2* (P21+ target), leaving viewports with direct-manipulation\n  fixtures only. Today's ribbon already satisfies this; the leftover floating toolbar\n  concept should not be resurrected.\n- **Durability:** **[D]** as a location and as a grammar (a segmented axis pair + a\n  contextual action run). **[P24]** for the Scene 3D `Move/Rotate/Scale/Add Asset/\n  Local|World` group and for any staging action that P24 adds or reorganizes.\n  **[P26]** for the `Plan|3D` pair and for any future view-local display control.\n\n#### Left rail `[D]` in role, **[P2…15771 tokens truncated… 6.3 Color ownership\n\nDomain accents belong primarily to the Chassis.\n\nScene brass and Camera cyan must not flood the Paper.\n\nPaper owns spatial state colors such as selection, snap/guide and refusal.\n\nNo critical state may be communicated by hue alone.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Typography and iconography\n\nUse two typographic voices:\n\n- a warm humanist sans for ordinary UI;\n- a narrow mechanical mono for measurements, coordinates, IDs, references, timestamps and precision values.\n\nApproximate hierarchy:\n\n- 10 px: engraved/group labels;\n- 11 px: status and compact readouts;\n- 12 px: standard rows and labels;\n- 13 px: property values;\n- 15 px: panel headings;\n- up to 20 px only where project identity genuinely needs it.\n\nDo not make the product feel premium by inflating headings.\n\nExisting P23.12 and P23.13 iconography should be retained. Protected Plan authoring silhouettes stay protected. General shell icons should be normalized around the existing visual family rather than replaced wholesale with a new icon library.\n\n---\n\n# 8. Domain Spine\n\nThe 56 px vertical Spine is the exclusive home of the primary domain axis.\n\nIt contains two persistent stations:\n\n- Scene;\n- Camera.\n\nThe active station receives a restrained **3 px inboard edge-light** using the domain accent.\n\nDo not use full-surface domain-color fills.\n\nDo not put Settings, Assets, Help or unrelated utilities into the reserved lower Spine merely because space exists.\n\nThe empty lower Spine is deliberate breathing room and future capacity, not an invitation to toolbar accretion.\n\n---\n\n# 9. Project Head\n\nThe Project Head is approximately 36 px high and remains compact.\n\nIt holds project/global context such as:\n\n`Projects → project name → session state → Undo/Redo → save state → Spatial / Publish → Preview → Theme → Account → Help`\n\nSpatial and Publish are independent chassis actions, not one segmented control.\n\nDo not create a second global toolbar.\n\n---\n\n# 10. View Bar\n\nThe View Bar spans only the central work column.\n\nPlan and 3D are engraved view tabs integrated into the Chassis. They must not look like ordinary tool buttons.\n\nScene Plan may additionally expose the subordinate MODE label with Layout / Arrange.\n\nUtility controls such as Snap, Grid, route visibility and Panels live in the same horizontal work-context region but are visually subordinate to the durable view tabs.\n\nThe View Bar must not extend over Navigator or Inspector.\n\n---\n\n# 11. Tool Tray\n\nThe Tool Tray is a **44 px vertical instrument rail attached directly to the Paper edge**.\n\nIt is not a second sidebar and must not become one.\n\nUse small persistent group labels and compact icon-led tools.\n\nRepresentative groups:\n\n### Scene Plan\n\n- DRAW: Wall, Rect Room, Poly Room\n- OPENINGS: Door, Window\n- OBJECTS: Place, Display, Sculpture, Seating, Platform, Column\n\n### Scene 3D\n\n- SELECT\n- TRANSFORM: Move, Rotate, Scale\n- SPACE: Local, World\n- OBJECTS: relevant placement actions\n\n### Camera Plan\n\n- CAMERA: Select, Add Camera, Connect, Sequence, Play\n\n### Camera 3D\n\n- CAMERA: Select, Move, Look At, Play\n\nThe exact tool list remains subject to product capability, but the rail grammar and density do not.\n\n---\n\n# 12. Navigator — recursive spatial hierarchy\n\nThis is the largest intentional evolution beyond Designer A’s original proposal.\n\nThe Navigator is **not** a flat inventory and **not** a filesystem.\n\nIt presents two related structures:\n\n1. **canonical spatial containment**;\n2. **contextual projections** of entities relevant to that spatial context.\n\n## 12.1 Future-facing containment model\n\nThe hierarchy must support structures such as:\n\n`Site → Building → Floor → Room/Space → Content`\n\nand more generally:\n\n`container → child container → child container → entities`\n\nThe product must not permanently hard-code “Museum → Rooms” as the only useful organization.\n\nExamples that should fit without a shell redesign:\n\n- Museum → Floor → Gallery;\n- House → Floor → Room;\n- Campus → Building → Floor → Exhibition Wing → Gallery;\n- future user-defined spatial containers if introduced later.\n\n## 12.2 Contextual projections\n\nA Room/Space may expose contextual groups such as:\n\n- Architecture;\n- Walls;\n- Openings;\n- Junctions;\n- Content;\n- Scene References;\n- future typed relationships.\n\nVisual nesting does **not** by itself establish canonical ownership.\n\nExample:\n\n`Gallery North → Architecture → Walls → W-FJSK`\n\nmeans “show the canonical wall relevant to Gallery North,” not necessarily “Gallery North owns this wall.”\n\nA shared wall may appear through multiple Room/Space contexts while resolving to the same canonical entity, reference, name and selection.\n\nThis principle must remain true for future multi-floor and cross-context features.\n\n## 12.3 Row species\n\nThe Navigator must visually distinguish:\n\n1. spatial/container entity;\n2. ordinary entity;\n3. typed group heading;\n4. contextual/reference occurrence;\n5. relation metadata;\n6. authored empty/teaching state.\n\nGroup rows such as Architecture, Walls or Content must not masquerade as ordinary selectable domain entities unless they actually become real domain entities.\n\n## 12.4 Identity presentation\n\nNamed entities lead with name and preserve the complete compact reference.\n\nUnnamed Wall/Opening/Junction entities may lead with reference according to P23.12.\n\nReferences never truncate.\n\nNames may ellipsize under pressure, but the Inspector must expose the full name.\n\n## 12.5 Depth and density\n\nDeep hierarchy must remain usable at 268 px reference width.\n\nUse shallow indentation—approximately 10–12 px per level—not oversized folder-tree indentation.\n\nAs depth increases, preserve:\n\n- disclosure affordance;\n- entity kind cues;\n- useful name excerpt;\n- full compact reference;\n- active selection;\n- search/reveal context.\n\nMetadata and decorative counts degrade before identity.\n\n---\n\n# 13. Inspector\n\nThe Inspector remains property-first.\n\nIt is not a dashboard, tutorial, project summary or documentation surface.\n\nThe top selection header should expose:\n\n- type icon;\n- name or reference;\n- secondary reference when named;\n- kind.\n\nSections follow entity semantics, for example:\n\n- Geometry;\n- Transform;\n- Placement;\n- Identity;\n- Relationships;\n- Lens;\n- Sequence;\n- Actions.\n\nConsequential/destructive actions come last.\n\nP23.12 rename authority remains Inspector-owned.\n\nTechnical/raw canonical IDs remain behind the P23.12 Technical details disclosure rather than leaking into normal identity surfaces.\n\n---\n\n# 14. Status Rail\n\nThe Status Rail is approximately 24 px high and is **readout-only**.\n\nIt may report:\n\n- current domain and view;\n- current selection/reference;\n- save state;\n- grid/snap/metric state;\n- coordinates or other low-noise work-state readouts.\n\nIt must not duplicate toolbar actions.\n\nOne fact should have one authoritative control owner. Status may passively echo a fact but should not create a second control.\n\n---\n\n# 15. Scene states\n\n## 15.1 Scene / Plan / Layout\n\nThe Plan remains the strongest demonstration of PLATE:\n\n- landed P23.13 Plan Paper `#F5F7F8`;\n- cool Chassis;\n- vertical Scene domain station;\n- horizontal Plan view tab;\n- vertical Tool Tray;\n- recursive Navigator;\n- property-first Inspector.\n\nP23.13 owns architectural Plan representation. P23.14 owns the surrounding shell and state language.\n\nSelection must reconcile across Navigator, Plan, Inspector and Status.\n\n## 15.2 Scene / Plan / Arrange\n\nArrange remains a subordinate Scene Plan local mode, not a new top-level view.\n\n## 15.3 Scene / 3D\n\nSwitching Plan → 3D must feel like changing the representation of the same work, not launching a different application.\n\nThe shell, Navigator and Inspector stay stable. The Tool Tray vocabulary changes to 3D-relevant instruments.\n\nThe 3D viewport remains an editor Paper surface—not a visitor presentation mode.\n\n---\n\n# 16. Camera states\n\nCamera uses the same shell grammar but changes work ownership and content.\n\n## 16.1 Camera / Plan\n\nScene architecture is passive spatial context.\n\nThe Camera graph/sequence overlays receive attention priority.\n\nCamera Plan may show:\n\n- camera nodes;\n- route/sequence;\n- direction;\n- selected camera;\n- ordering.\n\nIt must **not** show a finite FOV/frustum cone.\n\nAny Camera Graph shown beneath a Floor/Space in the Navigator is a contextual presentation unless/until a separate product contract establishes canonical ownership. Do not infer that a tour or sequence is permanently owned by one floor; future tours may cross floors.\n\n## 16.2 Camera / 3D\n\nA finite frustum for the selected camera is appropriate in Camera 3D.\n\nRoute context may remain visible when useful.\n\nThe viewport remains an editor view.\n\n---\n\n# 17. Camera Drawer and Timeline\n\nThe Camera Drawer belongs to the Camera domain and spans the central work column only.\n\n## 17.1 Collapsed\n\nReference height: **48 px**.\n\nShow compact sequence transport/readout only.\n\nNo fake lanes. No ruler.\n\n## 17.2 Expanded\n\nReference height: **288 px**.\n\nUse exactly these five semantic lanes, in this order:\n\n1. Camera Path\n2. Shots\n3. FOV\n4. Look At\n5. Roll\n\nThe Timeline must feel native to the same PLATE instrument system.\n\nDo not introduce:\n\n- audio tracks;\n- object-animation tracks;\n- generic video-editor track taxonomies;\n- storyboard-thumbnail substitution for the semantic lanes;\n- duplicate scrubbers.\n\nRoll is a quiet compressed summary row, explicitly showing 0° when unchanged. It should not look like a waveform.\n\nNo-flow, edge and sequence states are states of the same Camera surface, not separate products.\n\n---\n\n# 18. Selection and state language\n\nThe visual system must clearly distinguish at minimum:\n\n- hover;\n- selected;\n- primary selection;\n- keyboard focus;\n- active domain;\n- active durable view;\n- active local mode;\n- armed tool;\n- disabled;\n- refusal/invalid;\n- warning;\n- destructive action;\n- preview/ghost;\n- snap/guide;\n- authored versus derived information.\n\nDo not overload selection blue to mean every state.\n\nDo not depend on hue alone.\n\nSelection coherence is a signature product behavior: Navigator ↔ viewport ↔ Inspector ↔ Timeline should feel like one identity moving through different representations.\n\n---\n\n# 19. Precision and guidance\n\nPrecision feedback should appear close to the gesture that caused it.\n\nExamples:\n\n- dimensions near selected/drawn geometry;\n- snap indication at the snap location;\n- refusal reason near the failed action when practical;\n- numeric precision in Inspector or local direct-entry surfaces.\n\nDo not make the Status Rail carry all precision communication.\n\nEducational guidance should be contextual and authored:\n\n- useful empty states;\n- short reason text for refusal;\n- labels that clarify hierarchy;\n- concise context help where needed.\n\nDo not turn the Inspector into documentation.\n\n---\n\n# 20. P24 pressure contract\n\nP23.14 must not pre-design P24 behavior, but it must leave room for P24 without shell reinvention.\n\nThe Atlas should pressure-test at least:\n\n- denser Scene content;\n- multiple selected objects;\n- object groups / staging relationships;\n- richer Inspector property sets;\n- materials and lights;\n- larger Navigator inventories.\n\nThe shell should absorb these by contextual density and progressive disclosure, not by adding another permanent toolbar or dashboard.\n\n---\n\n# 21. P26 contextual-instrument contract\n\nP23.14 must create a clear host for future contextual architectural instruments without deciding every P26 interaction today.\n\nFuture Section / Wall Elevation / Ceiling Focus should:\n\n- enter from an existing durable view;\n- replace or focus the central Paper surface;\n- show their contextual identity visibly;\n- retain domain context;\n- retain selection coherence;\n- expose an obvious return path to the owning durable view;\n- avoid appearing as a third peer view beside Plan and 3D.\n\nThe Atlas should include at least one non-authoritative P26 stress specimen showing how a contextual instrument could occupy the shell while preserving this hierarchy.\n\nThis specimen is for pressure-testing only and must not invent P26 product semantics.\n\n---\n\n# 22. Responsive and density behavior\n\nPLATE must survive professional density rather than only hero screenshots.\n\nRequired stress cases include:\n\n- Navigator width 240–300 px;\n- 3 floors;\n- 8+ rooms/spaces per floor;\n- 70+ walls total;\n- deeply expanded Room/Space → Architecture → Walls branch;\n- long authored names;\n- duplicate names distinguished by references;\n- a wall exposed from more than one Room/Space context;\n- 8+ cameras;\n- expanded 288 px Camera Timeline;\n- smaller desktop height around 768 px;\n- light and alternate-theme contrast checks if alternate themes remain.\n\nProgressive density is preferred over disappearance.\n\nWhen space tightens, remove or compress redundant metadata before hiding identity, selection or location.\n\n---\n\n# 23. Accessibility and motion\n\nThe Atlas and implementation must validate:\n\n- keyboard navigation;\n- visible focus independent from selection;\n- accessible disclosure controls;\n- accessible Inspector controls;\n- non-hue-only state differences;\n- readable contrast;\n- reduced-motion behavior;\n- pointer-target adequacy;\n- coarse-pointer fallback where relevant.\n\nMotion should reinforce hierarchy and continuity, not decorate the shell.\n\n---\n\n# 24. Explicit non-goals / rejected interpretations\n\nP23.14 must not:\n\n- turn Scene and Camera into separate applications;\n- turn Plan/3D into a generic dropdown that hides the durable-view distinction;\n- introduce Section/Elevation as a third durable peer view;\n- move the Inspector into a dashboard role;\n- make the Navigator a literal filesystem;\n- infer canonical ownership from tree placement;\n- permanently flatten Navigator into Rooms / Architecture / Placed Content sections;\n- add a second global toolbar;\n- widen the Tool Tray into another sidebar;\n- turn Camera Timeline into a generic video editor;\n- reopen P23.12 identity;\n- redraw settled P23.13 Plan iconography without cause;\n- make the old navy dark mode the default product identity;\n- use theme color as the main source of hierarchy;\n- add chrome simply because empty space exists.\n\n---\n\n# 25. Designer D — Atlas assignment\n\nDesigner D should now build or revise the interactive HTML Atlas against this document.\n\nThe Atlas is a **QA surface**, not a competing proposal.\n\nIts job is to make the design falsifiable before implementation and during visual acceptance.\n\n## 25.1 Required canonical specimens\n\nThe Atlas must include four high-fidelity canonical states:\n\n### A. Scene / Plan / Layout\n\n- recursive multi-floor Navigator;\n- one expanded Space/Room → Architecture → Walls branch;\n- selected wall using P23.12 identity;\n- P23.13 Plan representation;\n- property-first Inspector;\n- landed P23.13 Plan Paper `#F5F7F8` / cool PLATE Chassis.\n\n### B. Scene / 3D\n\nLayout/Arrange remain Scene Plan local modes; Scene 3D has no parallel local-mode control.\n\n- same shell geometry;\n- same recursive hierarchy;\n- selected Scene object;\n- 3D transform instruments;\n- no shell redesign between Plan and 3D.\n\n### C. Camera / Plan / collapsed Drawer\n\n- passive architecture;\n- selected camera graph node;\n- no finite frustum;\n- collapsed 48 px Camera Drawer;\n- Camera hierarchy uses the same Navigator grammar;\n- any floor placement is explicitly contextual, not canonical ownership.\n\n### D. Camera / 3D / expanded Drawer\n\n- selected camera + finite frustum;\n- expanded 288 px Drawer;\n- exactly Camera Path / Shots / FOV / Look At / Roll;\n- no generic video-editor tracks;\n- coherent selection across Navigator / viewport / Inspector / Timeline.\n\n## 25.2 Required stress specimens\n\nThe Atlas should also expose interactive stress toggles or dedicated fixtures for:\n\n- deep multi-floor hierarchy;\n- long names + complete compact references;\n- duplicate names;\n- same canonical Wall revealed in two Space contexts;\n- 70+ wall density;\n- 8+ cameras;\n- expanded Timeline at reduced vertical height;\n- empty state;\n- disabled/refusal/warning/destructive states;\n- keyboard focus;\n- reduced motion;\n- future P26 contextual-instrument host.\n\n## 25.3 Atlas authority rule\n\nThe four owner-generated PLATE images may be used as visual orientation evidence for composition, warmth and intended density.\n\nThey are **not** pixel truth and must not be mined for accidental semantics.\n\nKnown examples of generator output that must not become product contracts include:\n\n- arbitrary fixture IDs beyond ratified identity rules;\n- Camera Graph appearing under a Floor as implied ownership;\n- inconsistent utility-control order;\n- generated room/wall relationship mistakes;\n- exact typography/icon deviations from existing P23.12/P23.13 assets.\n\nThe Atlas should correct these against this document.\n\n---\n\n# 26. Acceptance criteria\n\nP23.14 visual implementation is ready for acceptance when the Atlas and product make the following true simultaneously:\n\n1. A user can identify project, domain, durable view, local mode and active tool without those ranks competing visually.\n2. Scene/Camera remain visibly one product.\n3. Plan/3D remain durable peers.\n4. Paper, Chassis and Instrument are visually distinct without decorative excess.\n5. The new PLATE Light theme is the canonical default and the old navy dark identity is no longer the product baseline.\n6. Navigator supports recursive spatial containment and contextual projections without implying false ownership.\n7. Multi-floor hierarchy does not require a new shell.\n8. P23.12 identity remains intact through Navigator, Inspector, search and selection.\n9. P23.13 Plan representation/iconography remains intact.\n10. Selection is coherent across every visible surface.\n11. Inspector stays property-first.\n12. Status stays readout-only.\n13. Camera Timeline remains Camera-owned and central-work-column aligned.\n14. Camera Plan contains no finite frustum; Camera 3D may.\n15. Expanded Camera Timeline uses exactly the five ratified semantic lanes.\n16. P24 growth can be accommodated without adding permanent chrome.\n17. P26 can enter as subordinate contextual instruments with a visible return path.\n18. The shell survives dense, nested and reduced-height stress fixtures.\n19. Keyboard, focus, contrast and reduced-motion checks pass.\n20. The result still feels recognizably like Museum Editor—evolved, not replaced.\n\n---\n\n# 27. Final design statement\n\nP23.14 should leave Museum Editor with a shell that is recognizable before its palette is visible.\n\nIts signature is:\n\n**spatial Paper (landed cool P23.13 Paper in Plan; warm PLATE Paper elsewhere) held inside a cool engineered Chassis, with compact Instruments attached directly to the work; Scene/Camera running vertically, Plan/3D running horizontally, and one coherent identity moving through Navigator, viewport, Inspector and Timeline.**\n\nThe Navigator scales from today’s Rooms to tomorrow’s Buildings, Floors, Spaces and contextual architecture without confusing navigation with ownership.\n\nThe new PLATE Light theme becomes the default face of the product. Existing P23.12 identity and P23.13 drafting/iconography remain foundations rather than collateral damage from polish.\n\nThis is the direction Designer D should now make concrete and falsifiable in the Atlas.\n"}

Authority and intent\n\nThis document is the final P23.14 design direction. It supersedes the earlier proposal-level interpretations of PLATE while retaining the strongest parts of Designer A’s concept.\n\nThe final direction is a synthesis of:\n\n- the P23.14 research and shell-design context;\n- Designer A’s PLATE proposal;\n- owner ratification and visual exploration;\n- current Museum Editor shell behavior;\n- P23.12 identity/search/Inspector contracts;\n- P23.13 Plan drafting and iconography contracts;\n- P24 staging and multi-selection pressure;\n- P26 contextual architectural-instrument pressure;\n- the four ratified PLATE visual specimens: Scene/Plan, Scene/3D, Camera/Plan, Camera/3D + Timeline.\n\nDesigner A remains the conceptual ancestor of the material and axis grammar, but this document is now the authority.\n\nThe Atlas produced from this document is **not** the product specification. It is an interactive, falsifiable QA instrument that demonstrates the specification. If an Atlas detail conflicts with this document, this document wins.\n\n---\n\n# 1. Product thesis\n\nP23.14 should make Museum Editor feel like a **professional spatial-authoring instrument with a warm working surface held inside a cool engineered chassis**.\n\nThe shell should feel evolved from the current product rather than replaced by a new application. Existing mental models that already work should remain recognizable: hierarchy on the left, work in the center, properties on the right, Scene/Camera as domain context, Plan/3D as durable views, and Camera Timeline as a Camera-owned surface.\n\nThe visual identity must come from structure, material, typography, state and interaction—not decoration.\n\nThe product should feel:\n\n- professional;\n- architectural;\n- warm;\n- creative;\n- precise;\n- information-rich;\n- stable;\n- educational when context is needed;\n- distinctive without becoming theatrical.\n\nIt must not become:\n\n- generic SaaS;\n- literal CAD cosplay;\n- a floating-card dashboard;\n- a sci-fi control room;\n- a video-editing application;\n- a minimal shell that hides useful structure;\n- a theme whose identity depends mainly on color.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Core product contracts\n\nThese are not open for redesign in P23.14.\n\n## 2.1 One product, one world\n\nScene and Camera are attention domains over the same project/world. They are not separate documents or applications.\n\n## 2.2 Durable top-level views\n\nPlan and 3D remain the durable top-level views.\n\nP26 contextual orthographic instruments—such as Section, Wall Elevation and Ceiling Focus—must be subordinate contextual instruments entered from a durable view with an obvious return path. They must not become a third peer view or a new top-level world.\n\n## 2.3 Camera Timeline ownership\n\nThe Camera Timeline belongs only to the Camera domain and is available in both Camera Plan and Camera 3D.\n\nIt is part of the central work surface, not a global application footer and not a Scene feature.\n\n## 2.4 Scene Plan local modes\n\nLayout and Arrange remain Scene Plan local modes. Camera does not gain a parallel local-mode system merely for visual symmetry.\n\n## 2.5 One selection authority\n\nNavigator, viewport, Inspector, search/reveal, Timeline and status readouts must resolve to the same canonical selection identity.\n\nA selected object must never appear to be one thing in the Navigator and another thing in the Inspector.\n\n## 2.6 P23.12 identity contract survives\n\nP23.14 consumes P23.12 identity. It does not reopen it.\n\nNames, compact references, canonical identity, search behavior and Inspector rename authority remain as ratified in P23.12.\n\n## 2.7 P23.13 drafting/iconography contract survives\n\nExisting ratified Plan drafting language and protected P23.13 iconography remain intact unless an implementation defect requires correction.\n\nP23.14 may harmonize surrounding shell presentation but must not casually redraw or reinterpret the settled Plan authoring silhouettes.\n\nThe landed owner ruling retains Wall / Rect Room / Poly Room / Door / Window toolbar icons as Lucide `BrickWall` / `Square` / `Pentagon` / `DoorOpen` / `Grid2x2`. The Plan Door drawing cue remains perpendicular three-dash ink; it is distinct from the retained toolbar Door icon. Other integrated P23.13 drafting marks retain their settled paths.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Information-rank grammar\n\nThe shell should make this rank legible:\n\n**Project → Domain → View → Contextual instrument → Local mode → Tool → Selection / gesture / status**\n\nEach rank has a different visual job.\n\n- **Project** is persistent application context.\n- **Domain** answers whether attention is on Scene or Camera.\n- **View** answers whether the durable representation is Plan or 3D.\n- **Contextual instrument** answers whether the user has entered a subordinate focused representation such as a future P26 Section.\n- **Local mode** changes how the current view is being edited, such as Layout or Arrange.\n- **Tool** is armed action.\n- **Selection / gesture / status** describes the immediate target, interaction or readout.\n\nDo not flatten these ranks into controls with equal visual weight.\n\nThe primary signature is perpendicular:\n\n- **Scene / Camera = vertical domain axis**\n- **Plan / 3D = horizontal view axis**\n\nThat relationship should remain visible even when the user is not consciously thinking about it.\n\n---\n\n# 4. PLATE material grammar\n\nPLATE uses exactly three visual-material classes.\n\n## 4.1 CHASSIS\n\nPersistent application structure:\n\n- Domain Spine;\n- Project Head;\n- Navigator;\n- Inspector;\n- View Bar;\n- Status Rail;\n- Camera Drawer shell.\n\nThe Chassis is cool, stable, square and quiet.\n\n## 4.2 PAPER\n"}

Canonical output deep-clones and emits the envelope only when authored; absence stays absent and preserves legacy bytes. Standalone scene codec validates room IDs syntactically plus envelope shape/order; project codec owns cross-document room-reference + world-space camera-pose validation and repeats envelope range/order checks at export. P20 S0 keeps `SceneTextureAsset = { id, name, uri }`; static safe URIs remain public resources, `/local/...` and package rewrites are session/package-only, and `/project-assets/{assetId}` is a logical authenticated registry reference resolved by the editor app.\n\n**Layout:** canonical `units: 'meters'`, floors → rooms → stable room frame + draft paths → openings → layout objects. No version field; rooms always carry a finite `frame.origin: [x, z]` + normalized radian `frame.yaw`. Opening `offset` = meters along a segment, never a sample index. Door-only `connectsRoomIds: [string, string]` relations explicit, validated, never inferred from geometry; windows unpaired. The authored layout contract and renderer-neutral compiler live in `@portfolio/layout-core`; app layout paths are compatibility facades where retained.\n\n**Editor layout session:** canonical `serializeLayoutDocument()` output = layout baseline. Status = `blank | dirty | imported`; invalid-import feedback independent, preserves prior layout, model, selection, source, baseline, dirty state. Layout import/copy/download/reset operate only on layout JSON (`layout.json`). Scene replacement checks scene dirtiness, layout replacement checks layout dirtiness, navigation/unload checks both. Room-unit relocation moves frame, boundary, owned objects atomically in one tagged `layout` history entry; vertex, bend, opening edits leave frame unchanged. Editor may hold intentionally independent transient scene/layout documents, so preview state validates each edited domain without requiring every intermediate pair to form valid project.\n\n**Layout object transforms:** `position` = world/layout center; Euler `rotation` = radians; `dimensions` = full local extents before rotation. Plan footprints + bounds use rotation-aware world AABBs. Authored `box | plane | cylinder | sphere` objects editable; imported `profile` objects round-trip unchanged, read-only in A4.\n\n**Project:** `{ id, name, layout, scene }`. Visitor-safe shared codec validates nested layout + scene in one shape, prefixes nested issues, rejects scene room references absent from same layout. `chopin-project.json` = sole production layout/scene source; `chopin-project.ts` validates once, exposes project, one room registry, resolved scene, navigation graph, runtime. No assets, binary payloads, UI, or history in envelope. Portable package IDs, manifest/filename rules, and `sha256Bytes` also live in `@portfolio/project-model`; ZIP/Blob orchestration stays in editor import/export.\n\n**Cloud persistence (P19 shipped 2026-09-03):** authenticated owned Save/Load wraps the same canonical `ProjectDocument` — `projects` row + `project_versions` versioned JSONB. Save validates the full document, locks the project row, appends a version, and bumps `latest_version`; Load reads the latest version and revalidates; the Hub lists owned projects with versions. Save takes no expected-base-version precondition today — row locking serializes writes but a stale full document can still save as a newer version; a stale-write/revision precondition is a future trigger before simultaneous human/agent writers, not current behavior.\n\n**Project asset durability (P20 shipped 2026-09-04):** registry metadata in Postgres, heavy texture bytes in private R2 through `apps/api` only. Session/package texture URIs cannot reach semantic JSONB Save (durability gate); package export resolves bytes and embeds them. Assets are not normalized into `ProjectDocument` — the registry/storage boundary stays separate.\n\n**Publish + releases (P22 shipped 2026-09-08):** one `publications` row per project (unique project ID, unique random public ID allocated only by first Publish, nullable active version, monotonic publication revision, timestamps) plus immutable `releases` keyed by `(project_id, version)` carrying the validated delivery manifest (saved `ProjectDocument` snapshot + pinned P20 asset key/SHA-256/MIME/size + shipped-static compatibility projection). Publish/Update/Unpublish run a short SQL transaction (row lock, ownership + expected-revision + saved-version recheck, release insert/reuse, active-pointer switch, revision bump); R2 verification (bounded streaming hash/size/MIME check) happens before the transaction, never inside it. First publish alone allocates the stable public ID; status GET never creates one. ABA (`N → unpublished → N`) still bumps revision, so stale writers get 409. Anonymous reads resolve the active release once per bootstrap (document + manifest share one version); unpublish disables all document/byte delivery for that public ID. `Cache-Control: no-store` on public metadata/bytes. Owner API: `GET/PUT/DELETE /projects/:id/publication`, anonymous: `GET /publications/:id` + version-qualified asset content.\n\n**Asset policy:** portable packages may embed user texture bytes through their manifest; scene model entities still reference shipped catalogue `assetId`s only. Project-local GLB import remains deferred. P20 cloud Save uses a separate durability gate: session/package texture URIs cannot reach semantic JSONB Save, while package export resolves bytes and embeds them.\n\n**History:** one chronological undo stack; entries tagged `layout` | `scene`; 100-entry cap; scene/layout transactions validate before commit; undo/redo replaces only entry's domain; no-op skips entry; scene or layout import/reset clears stack. History is editor-session state only — never serialized, not a collaboration operation log, and separate per-domain entries are not an atomic cross-domain transaction.\n\n**Session-only:** `scaleVector` map, lighting overrides, UI chrome, pending ghosts.\n\n**Fidelity:** independent scale may be lossy for visitors; editor-only meshes desync until shared factory/bake; no auto repo writeback.\n"}

Tolerances are screen-px\n  constants divided by `pixelsPerMeter`.\n- **Edges:** every connection once, undirected, no arrowheads; retained\n  (non-flow) edges stay visible in a distinct style. Order changes numbering,\n  never the edge set.\n- **Order/free:** ordered nodes show stable `1…N` labels from\n  `store.mainFlowNodeIds`; unsequenced nodes show an unnumbered dashed ring and\n  “Unsequenced” in the inspector.\n- **Node colors (P21.5 Slice 2B):** node role colors are canvas-invariant\n  tokens — plan paper is theme-invariant, so node ink never follows the shell\n  theme. Sequenced nodes fill `#2F8CFF` with white bold tabular numerals and a\n  1.5px white ring; selected deepens to `#1976DF`, grows 24→28px, and gains a\n  translucent blue halo ring. Unsequenced nodes are paper discs with a 2px\n  dashed emerald ring and an emerald dot; when selected they take a blue tint\n  fill, a solid emerald ring, and the same halo. Selection uses halos + size,\n  never fill inversion, and picking uses its own screen-px hit radius — the\n  24→28px growth is presentation-only.\n- **3D camera visualization (P21.6 Slices A/B):** 3D nodes/paths/frustums are\n  authored spatial truth, invariant in every theme (`SCENE_PALETTE` camera\n  tokens, never chrome accent). Sequenced 3D cores fill `#2F8CFF` with an\n  HTML numbered badge (blue disc, 1.5px white keyline, white tabular numeral);\n  unsequenced cores are transparent emerald with an emerald perimeter ring\n  (billboarded); selection adds a concentric `#1976DF` ring, never fill\n  inversion. Paths run `#2F8CFF` (selected `#55A1FF`); retained edges keep the\n  gray dashed language. The selected camera shows a translucent `#2F8CFF`\n  frustum fill (9%, scene-occluded) with a 1px blue perimeter, a dark apex\n  nub at the eye, compact blue FOV handles, a dashed slate look-at ray to a\n  target reticle, and off-white anchor dots for the selected connection only.\n  Picking rides invisible shells (`colorWrite:false + depthWrite:false +\n  opacity:1`) clamped to a 24px minimum projected diameter; same-class\n  overlaps arbitrate by closest projected center under the existing\n  nodes > view-keyframes > anchors > connections priority. FOV drags freeze\n  their basis at pointer-down (signed top/bottom identity, true grab offset)\n  with a vertical-slider fallback below 0.15 alignment — one undoable\n  gesture, invalid intersections hold the last valid FOV. Paused Director\n  preview yields the frustum to an editable node/view-keyframe selection;\n  paused with no editable selection owns `none` (clean viewport, never a\n  resurrected wireframe); playback keeps the playhead owner. Frame off\n  suppresses both frustum implementations; an ineligible selection helper\n  (wrong workspace / pending placement) cannot suppress the playhead — no\n  stop/seek side effects. Arbitration covers camera handles only\n  (placements/architecture keep distance order) with winning-class-first +\n  CSS-pixel order shared by hover/click/drag-entry; per-move FOV alignment\n  rechecks hold grazing blowups; hidden helpers never pick; nub + preview\n  orient from the shared framing basis.\n- **Anchors:** interior anchors render only for the selected connection;\n  dragging an edge with no anchor inserts one at the nearest curve progress\n  (shared `getCameraPathInsertionIndex` / `insertConnectionAnchorAtWorldPoint`\n  ownership rules) then drags it. Authored node/anchor Y is preserved\n  byte-for-byte; snap adjusts X/Z only.\n- **Timing:** each edge shows one compact pill at the spline midpoint with\n  durations only — `4.2s` when both directions resolve the same readout,\n  otherwise `4.2s · 3.8s` (forward · reverse); non-finite reads `—`. Pill ink\n  is canvas-invariant (the Plan canvas is paper in every theme — never the\n  chrome accent): unselected pills are white with a slate border + charcoal\n  ink so they read on paper and room floors; the selected edge's pill takes a\n  deep-sapphire fill + white ink with an electric-blue perimeter tied to the\n  route. Automatic segments carry a dotted underline (3px offset) + tooltip,\n  authored segments render solid. Node names,\n  direction breakdown, and derived speed live in the connection Inspector.\n  The Camera Plan connection inspector authors `durationSeconds` per direction\n  through `setConnectionTiming` (finite-positive validation, one undo step,\n  “Use automatic” removes only duration, preserving easing). Path length,\n  effective duration, and derived speed come from the exact per-direction\n  `CameraMotion` the timeline constructs — never UI-local math.\n- **History:** add, connect, delete, node drag, anchor insert/drag, and timing\n  edits each produce one scene-history entry; selection/hover/pan/zoom/toolbar\n  changes and failed/cancelled gestures produce none. Escape cancels an active\n  drag first (capture-phase, cannot fall through to pending-command\n  cancellation), then the pending navigation command, then returns to Select.\n- **Preview grammar (P12):** ordinary Camera/connection selection is\n  selection-only. `Preview Camera` explicitly enters paused static `Camera`\n  scope for an Unsequenced node; `Preview Edge` explicitly enters paused local\n  `Edge` scope with deterministic direction; the Scope menu explicitly enters\n  `Sequence`. None autoplays. A sequenced-node click seeks + pauses only while\n  Sequence scope is already active. Active scope labels stay compact (`Camera ·\n  C`, `Edge · B → C`, `Sequence (Full Tour)`). Connections remain one undirected\n  topology model; direction is traversal/preview state. Safe authoring pauses a\n  playing preview in place. Main-editor transport is binary Play/Pause; Play at\n  end restarts from `0`, Escape pauses without tearing down, and Repeat/loop /\n  distinct Replay chrome is absent. Edge Repeat and stop-on-Escape remain only\n  in the frozen relic.\n\nVisitor: plays the open-chain order (loop derived); free nodes via BFS; transitioning = no nav; Paris = fixed eye + free-look. No ribbons on `/museum`.\n\nLimits: no collision/navmesh; synthesized look; timeline drag-connect ≤1 new edge; guarded deletes.  \nFuture Experience / Interaction consumers reference this canonical camera\ngraph/route/motion evaluation; they do not implement another one.\nzsh:2: no matches found: packages/project-model/src/*navigation*\n"}

Use when substantial work is interrupted mid-task and another agent must continue the same unit without reconstructing the investigation.\n---\n\n# Work Checkpoint\n\nTransient recovery state for unfinished work.\nApplies to: debugging, research, architecture investigation, implementation, planning.\n\n## Create\n\n- Only when substantial work is interrupted and expensive to reconstruct.\n- One checkpoint per active interrupted task: `docs/operations/checkpoints/<task>.md`.\n- Same task → update existing file in place. Never `*-2.md`, `*-final.md`.\n\n## Procedure\n\n```text\nCREATE:\n- no checkpoint for this task → write schema below\n\nUPDATE:\n- same task has checkpoint → overwrite stale frontier\n- preserve still-valid ESTABLISHED / EVIDENCE / RULED OUT\n\nPOINT:\n- current.md RESUME → checkpoint path\n- NEXT = resume checkpoint\n\nCOMPLETE:\n- promote durable findings\n- delete checkpoint\n- remove RESUME pointer\n- advance current.md if clean boundary reached\n```\n\n## Schema\n\n```text\n# <task> — checkpoint\n\nTYPE:\nSTATUS: paused\nGOAL:\n\nREAD:\n- exact files/docs already relevant\n\nESTABLISHED:\n- confirmed facts only\n\nEVIDENCE:\n- path/symbol/test/commit anchors\n\nRULED OUT:\n- disproven hypotheses\n\nDO NOT REPEAT:\n- investigation already completed\n\nCURRENT:\n- present reasoning frontier / partial implementation state\n\nNEXT:\n1. exact next action\n2. exact next action\n\nOPEN:\n- unresolved questions\n```\n\nOmit irrelevant fields.\n\n## Rules\n\n```text\nESTABLISHED ≠ hypothesis\nRULED OUT = actually disproven\nCURRENT = current frontier, not narrative history\nNEXT = 1–3 exact continuation actions\n```\n\nNo chain-of-thought prose. Conclusions, evidence, decisions, next actions only.\n\n## Resume\n\n- Read current.md → checkpoint.\n- Inspect live Git state.\n- Open only checkpoint READ / EVIDENCE targets.\n- Continue from NEXT.\n\n## Complete\n\nPromote before delete:\n\n```text\nbug deferred → operations/tech-debt/\nresearch completed → owning research/synthesis artifact\nlanded behavior → reference/*\nverification/rulings → closeout/archive\nimplementation status → phase/slice README\n```\n\nDo not archive raw checkpoints.\n"}

{"chunk_id":"4e0f31","wall_time_seconds":0.000002459,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4510,"output":"# Tech-debt ledger — deferred defects\n\n**Audience:** the implementer of a future slice (today: **P24**) plus whoever triages\na bug report against this repository.\n**Purpose:** record a defect that is **understood, reproduced and deliberately not\nfixed yet** — with the evidence needed to fix it without re-deriving it.\n**Not** a plan, not a status tracker and not current working-tree state: those live in\n[`docs/roadmap/README.md`](../../roadmap/README.md) and\n[`docs/operations/current.md`](../current.md).\n\nEntry template:\n\n```text\n## TD-n — <one-line defect>\nStatus        open | fixed (<commit>) | superseded\nFound         date + how it was found\nDefer to      slice + why it is not urgent\nSymptom       user-visible behaviour, exactly as observed\nReproduce     recipe (and the probe that proved it, if temporary)\nRoot cause    files/lines, layered primary → latent\nBroke when    commit evidence\nWhy green     which tests would have had to exist to catch it\nFix options   options with tradeoffs + the option that must NOT be taken\nThen add      acceptance tests the fix owes\n```\n\n---\n\n## TD-1 — Floor-supported placement is unreachable in canonical (wall-first) projects: no camera, primitive, light or asset can be placed\n\n**Status:** open — **deferred to P24**.\n**Found:** 2026-09-17, by manual test of *new project → draw a Room → Camera → Plan →\nAdd Camera*. Reported with a console dump of unrelated Svelte warnings.\n**Defer to:** P24. Nothing in P24 depends on it, but P24's placement work\n(`P24.2` floor support / resolution reasons) is the natural owner: this defect *is* the\nfloor-support contract failing on the canonical document format. **P23.14–P23.16 must\nnot \"fix\" it incidentally** — it is format semantics, not shell polish.\n\n### Symptom\n\nIn a project created by the editor's own **New Project** boot:\n\n- Ribbon `Add Camera` / `Add camera` → Toolbar arms and the status bar says\n  `Click any tagged room floor to place a camera`.\n- Clicking **inside a drawn Room** — in Camera · Plan or Camera · 3D — produces\n  `Click a tagged room floor` on the status bar and **creates nothing**. No camera node,\n  no history entry, no error, no toast. The click is simply refused.\n- The same refusal applies to the Scene-3D **primitive**, **light** and **asset**\n  placement branches. (Layout-level `Place` presets — Column / Platform / Plinth — are a\n  different path and still work.)\n- The Room itself is fine: it draws in Scene · Plan, Camera · Plan and 3D, is selectable,\n  and the Plan hit-test resolves it.\n\n**Why it looks like \"the Timeline lanes show up differently\"** (the report this came\nfrom): with zero nodes there is no camera flow, so the Camera Timeline renders its\nno-sequence state (header + empty `288px` body, `No sequence yet`) and never its lane\nsurface — see [`../design/P23.14-shell-design-context.md`](../../roadmap/p23-layout-depth/p23.14-shell-visual-system/context/shell-design-context.md)\n§3. TD-1 is what keeps the node count at zero.\n\n### Reproduce\n\nFresh guest project (`/projects` → New Project), Scene · Plan · Layout → `Rect Room`,\ndrag a rectangle, then Camera · Plan → `Add Camera` → click the room interior.\nLegacy/room-first documents (the frozen `/museum/editor` relic, or any pre-P23 imported\nlayout) behave correctly — this is **format-gated**, not global.\n\nThe probe that proved it was a **temporary** vitest file\n(`tests/lib/editor/tmp-wallfirst-camera-placement.test.ts`, deleted after use) that built\na closed 4×3 boundary chain with `planWallChain(..., { close: true })` — one reconciled\nRoom — and asserted:\n\n```text\n✓ the wall-first room registry is empty (has/get false, frames throw)\n✓ the compiled Plan hit DOES resolve the room (so only the registry gate fails)\n× Camera Plan Add Camera can never commit a node in a wall-first document\n  Error: Unknown project layout room: room.5:4:face14:wall-chain-1-F14:…\n    ❯ Object.localPoint            packages/project-model/src/project-layout-semantics.ts:107\n    ❯ EditorNavigationGraphMutator.createPendingNavigationNodeAt\n                                   apps/editor/src/lib/editor/store/navigation-graph-mutator.svelte.ts:347\n```\n\n### Root cause\n\nTwo layers. The first is what the user sees; the second is what a naive fix would hit.\n\n**Layer 1 — the acceptance predicate is room-first, and the canonical format's room\nregistry is empty *by design*.**\n\n`createLayoutRoomRegistry(layout)` returns the **empty** registry for a wall-first\ndocument:\n\n- `packages/project-model/src/project-layout-semantics.ts:36` —\n  `if (isWallFirstLayout(layout)) return createEmptyLayoutRoomRegistry();`\n- `:84–88` states why: *\"A wall-first Layout carries no Room frames, and a world-local\n  Scene carries no `roomId` references (codec-enforced), so resolution never consults a\n  frame\"*. The empty registry's `has()` is `() => false`, `get()` is `() => undefined`,\n  and `point`/`localPoint`/`pointInFrame(roomId)` **throw**.\n- The editor installs exactly that registry at boot and after every layout mutation\n  (`apps/editor/src/lib/editor/app/EditorApp.svelte:1460`, `:1564` →\n  `store.updateRooms`), so re-deriving it can never help: **the emptiness is a function of\n  the document format, not of staleness.**\n\nThe two camera-placement consumers both gate on that registry:\n\n| Consumer | Gate |\n|---|---|\n| Camera · Plan — `apps/editor/src/lib/editor/camera-plan/CameraPlanViewport.svelte:272` (`beginPlaceCamera`) | `const entry = store.rooms.get(candidate.roomId); if (!entry) { setStatusMessage('Click a tagged room floor'); return; }` |\n| Camera · 3D — `apps/editor/src/lib/editor/EditorSelection.svelte:969–975` (place-camera branch) | `findPlaceableFloorIntersection(intersections, undefined, (id) => store.rooms.has(id))` → `null` → same message |\n\nNeither layer upstream is broken: the compiled geometry **does** produce the room-floor\npolygon (`findPlanHitRoom(geometry.queries, [2, 1.5])` returns the canonical room id —\n`packages/layout-core/src/layout-geometry.ts:1141`), and 3D **does** tag the floor mesh\n`editorSurface: { type: 'floor', placeable: true, roomId }`\n(`apps/editor/src/lib/editor/layout/LayoutPreviewScene.svelte:278`). Only the registry\nmembership check refuses.\n\n**Layer 2 — the placement command itself is room-first, so it cannot be repaired by\nloosening the predicate alone.**\n\n`apps/editor/src/lib/editor/store/navigation-graph-mutator.svelte.ts:342–349`:\n\n```ts\nconst node: SceneNavigationNode = {\n  id: nodeId,\n  roomId,                                             // ← Room ownership\n  …\n  position: this.host.rooms.localPoint(roomId, eyeWorld),      // ← throws today\n  cameraTarget: this.host.rooms.localPoint(roomId, targetWorld),\n```\n\nOn an empty registry `localPoint` throws `Unknown project layout room: …`\n(proved above). And even a \"successful\" room-owned write would be **rejected by the\nScene codec**: world-local documents must never carry Room ownership anywhere —\n`packages/project-model/src/scene-codec/index.ts:150–151`, `parse-document.ts:141–143`,\nenforced at the project level by `project-codec.ts:118` (`scene_not_world_local`) and\n`validateProjectSceneRooms`. The node would make the project unsaveable.\n\n### When it broke\n\nNot a regression from any single \"camera\" change: the **document format moved under the\nplacement code**, which was already room-first.\n\n| Commit | Date | Effect |\n|---|---|---|\n| `2c9b04d` — P23.0 F0 stage 4 | 2026-09-09 | `createLayoutRoomRegistry()` starts returning the **empty** registry for wall-first layouts → every `rooms.has/get/point` gate silently becomes `false`/`undefined`/throw |\n| `d1705b7` — P23.3 (PR #19) | 2026-09-10 | the canonical wall-first Layout + world-local Scene pair becomes the **New Project boot** → from here *every new project* is affected |\n| `69206f5` — P17/P18 | earlier | the `(id) => store.rooms.has(id)` predicate in `EditorSelection.svelte` predates all of this; it was simply never revisited |\n\nSo the user's \"since P23 began\" is exact: the defect is as old as the wall-first boot,\nand no P23 slice has had camera placement on its acceptance list.\n\n### Why the suite is green\n\n- `apps/editor/tests/lib/editor/app/live-rooms.test.ts` — the only test that pins camera\n  placement end-to-end — boots `createEmptyLayoutPreviewState()` (the **legacy** blank\n  document, *not* the canonical boot) and drafts a Room with `commitLayoutDraftRoom`\n  (legacy). It proves the registry seam works for room-first documents and says nothing\n  about the canonical pair.\n- `p23-3-new-project-boot.test.ts` covers the canonical boot but not placement.\n- Evidence-class warning, same shape as P23.13's residue: these tests pin *individual\n  seams* (`registry sync`, `predicate`, `createPendingNavigationNodeAt` in isolation).\n  No test crosses **canonical boot → placement → committed node**, which is the only\n  place the two layers meet. Add that crossing when TD-1 is fixed.\n\n### Not the cause: the `ownership_invalid_mutation` console warnings\n\nThe report included many Svelte warnings of the form *\"Mutating unbound props\n(`cameraPlan`, at `CameraPlanViewport.svelte:665:3`)\"* from `onPointerMove:664`,\n`onPointerLeave:788`, and the `onMount` writes at `:188`/`:204`. Those are real but\n**unrelated and harmless**: `cameraPlan` is a `$state` object owned by `EditorApp` and\npassed to `CameraPlanWorkspace` → `CameraPlanViewport` **by value**, so the child's\n`cameraPlan.hover = …` / `cameraPlan.planView` writes work but are flagged in dev. The\ndefect above reproduces with **zero** console output, because a refused placement is a\nstatus message, not an exception. Treat the warning as a separate, low-priority cleanup\n(e.g. `bind:cameraPlan` or moving the writes behind callbacks) — do **not** read it as\nthe bug.\n\n### Blast radius (canonical wall-first projects only)\n\n| Surface | Effect |\n|---|---|\n| Camera node placement — Plan and 3D | **dead** (`CameraPlanViewport.svelte:272`, `EditorSelection.svelte:969`) |\n| Scene primitive placement — 3D | **dead** (same predicate, `EditorSelection.svelte:992`) |\n| Scene light placement — 3D | **dead** (`EditorSelection.svelte:1011`) |\n| Scene asset placement — 3D | **dead** (`EditorSelection.svelte:1041`; message reads `Click a tagged room floor to place`) |\n| `store.focusRoom(id)` | always `false` (`editor-store.svelte.ts:2090`, `!this.rooms.has(id)`) |\n| `LayoutRoomRegistry.point/getRequired/localPoint` for a canonical room id | **throws** (`project-layout-semantics.ts:95–107`) — any new caller inherits this trap |\n| `isWorldPointInsideRoomXZ` (`editor-camera-path.ts:64`) | returns `false` (documented fallback: caller uses world ownership) — correct behaviour, not a defect |\n| Camera authoring *beyond* placement (connect, sequence, timing, framing, Timeline) | **unreachable**, because there is no way to author the first two nodes. Not itself broken |\n| Layout editing (`Rect Room`, Walls, Openings, `Place` presets, Arrange) | unaffected |\n| Plan/3D Room rendering, selection, Scene footprints | unaffected |\n\n### Fix options for P24\n\n**Option A — make placement world-local for canonical documents (recommended framing).**\nA wall-first-aware placement command that commits `roomId: undefined` and stores the\nalready-correct world-space eye/target (the identity frame is supported today:\n`pointInFrame(undefined, p) === p`), with the acceptance check moved from *registry\nmembership* to the evidence that already resolves — the compiled `room-floor` polygon in\nPlan, the `editorSurface`-tagged floor mesh in 3D. This is the direction the format\nalready declares (\"Scene/Camera physical placement is project/world-local\",\n`north-star.md` §1084–1087) and it is what P24.2's floor-support resolver needs anyway.\n\n**Option B — synthesize identity-frame registry entries for canonical rooms.** Cheaper\n(`createLayoutRoomRegistry` returns entries whose `floor` is the wall-first `floor` and\nwhose points are the world points), but **insufficient alone**: the placement command\nwould still write `roomId` into a world-local Scene, which the codec rejects. It would\nhave to be paired with stripping `roomId` — at which point it buys nothing over A, while\nkeeping room-first ownership semantics alive in a format that forbids them.\n\n**Must not do:** relax the Scene codec (or the wall-first Project validator) to accept\n`roomId` on world-local records, and do not \"fix\" the symptom by special-casing\n`rooms.has()` to return `true`. (2) The control polygon was derived as the centerline's endpoints, which is the true net only for a straight Wall: on a curve it rendered a straight chord over the curve it described, on precisely the Walls where the net matters. The net is now an **input** (the viewport reads junctions for a straight Wall and authored knots for a cubic chain) and an absent net draws no polygon — the helper never derives one. (3) `clearLayoutSelection` now releases focus too: a delete cleared selection but not focus, and because P23.12 recycles canonical IDs a reissued Junction/Opening id could resurrect a ring (plus its owner control net) on an entity never pressed. P23.12 identity labels for announcements.\ndocs/archive/plans/2026-09-16-P23.13-architectural-plan-drafting-finish.md:412:- **Done (step 4 — the acceptance map, driven live end to end).** The three keyboard legs that were only pinned are now **pressed**, in a fresh guest project with one 4-Wall room, a Door on the top Wall and a bend inserted into the bottom Wall (12.2 px/m, above the 6 px/m control gate). **Openings:** with the Door selected, **Enter** announced *\"Opening edge 1 of 3 — Opening O-XYAJ\"* with the ring at x 116.4; **ArrowRight** stepped to *\"Opening slide 2 of 3\"* (ring 127.3), again to *\"Opening edge 3 of 3\"* (ring 138.2), and a third time **wrapped** to edge 1 (ring back to 116.4) — the canonical start edge → slide → end edge order, with the S4 ring tracking each control and P23.12's own identity in every line. **Curves:** after *Add bend point at midpoint*, the same wall's group grew to **three** controls and read *\"Junction 1 of 3\"* → *\"Curve point 2 of 3\"* (ring at the knot) → *\"Junction 3 of 3\"* — the authored knot is reached in canonical order rather than skipped. **Enter → numeric:** Enter on the focused control opened the S7 field on the opening's own measure (*Width*, seeded `0.90`), **Tab** advanced to *Offset* (seeded `2.30`), and **Escape** closed the field with focus returned to the canvas (`svg.plan-canvas`) and **Rooms 1 / Walls 4 / Openings 1 unchanged** — one keyboard door from canvas to control to exact value, writing nothing on the way out. **Grayscale:** with the viewport filtered `grayscale(1)`, the walls, grid, selection, focus ring, dimension text and room label all stayed separable by luminance and shape, confirming the token arithmetic above by eye rather than only by number.\ndocs/archive/plans/2026-09-16-P23.13-architectural-plan-drafting-finish.md:414:- **Done (step 1 + 2 — control-group traversal and its announcement).** New pure module `plan-keyboard-traversal.ts` (same precedent as `plan-numeric-entry.ts`: the group is built from canonical facts the caller already holds, so its order cannot drift from the document) and the viewport wiring that owns focus. The contract, all of it enforced by absence rather than by a check: a **Wall** reads start junction → curve-knot ids in arc order → end junction; an **Opening** reads start edge → slide grip → end edge; a **Junction** is its own single control; **everything else** (rooms, objects, anchors, no selection, an unknown or missing id, a wall whose endpoints are not in the junction set) is `null` — so the keyboard can never focus a control the document cannot supply, which is the same refusal the acquisition engine makes. **Enter** first enters the selected owner's group at its first control (announced once) and a focused control's Enter then reaches S7's numeric door unchanged; a selection with no group keeps the resting-measure door directly, so nothing that worked before stops working. **Arrows** step the group with wrap — and *only* within a group the user has entered (see the 2026-09-17 review follow-up: the first cut let an arrow enter at the pressed end, which stole scrolling from a selected owner). **Escape** unwinds focus before anything coarser — and only after every gesture/draft branch has already returned, so it can never swallow a gesture cancel. Focus is **never** selection and **never** history: the focus move's whole body is `setPlanFocus` + one announcement, which `p23-13-keyboard` pins by source-slicing the function and asserting the absence of `selectLayout` / `clearLayoutSelection` / `onLayoutTransaction` / `commitLayout` / `History` inside it. Two gates keep it honest: traversal is offered only on a **quiet** canvas (no open field, no live gesture or draft) and only at the same LOD the overlay draws controls with (`JUNCTION_HANDLES_MIN_PX_PER_M`), because focusing a control the eye cannot see is the same lie as painting one. The announcement is **one** visually-hidden `role=\"status\"` region, written only by a keyboard move (role + position + owner identity from P23.12's labels, plus the focused control's current value and units — see the 2026-09-17 review follow-up) and released with the focus — pointer focus stays silent, so nothing speaks per pointermove. Pinned by the new `p23-13-keyboard` (21 after the review follow-ups — group order for line/curve/opening/junction, the null answers, stepping with wrap and in-group-only gating, the announcement string with and without a value, the control-kind boundary, and the viewport source contracts: the arrow gate, the Enter-before-numeric ordering, the Escape unwind position, the readout release helper and its reconciliation, the no-selection/no-history body, and the four `planAnnouncedFocus` writes).\ndocs/archive/plans/2026-09-16-P23.13-architectural-plan-drafting-finish.md:415:  **Live (2026-09-17, real editor, fresh guest project, one 4-Wall room at 12.2 px/m — above the 6 px/m control gate, so the group is offered at all).** With the Wall selected (`Layout selection` in the status bar) and the canvas focused, **Enter** entered the group and the live region read **\"Junction 1 of 2 — Wall W-7V24\"** — role, position and P23.12's own identity, exactly the contract. **ArrowRight** moved to \"Junction 2 of 2 — Wall W-7V24\", a second **ArrowRight** wrapped back to \"Junction 1 of 2\", and **ArrowLeft** walked back to the end — wrap confirmed in both directions, one announcement per move and nothing in between (the region is absent from the DOM at rest, so no pointermove can speak). The focus is **visible**, not just spoken: the `.focus-ring` overlay painted two circles (r 8.575 and 13.575) on the focused control and the *same* two circles moved to the other endpoint on the next arrow, which is the S4 focus overlay tracking the S10 focus state. **Escape** cleared the ring and the region and left the selection standing (`Layout selection` unchanged). **Focus wrote no history:** after four focus moves and the unwind, one **Undo** stepped the *geometry* back (1 room → 0, 4 walls → 3) rather than consuming an entry for a focus change — which is the live form of the \"focus is never history\" pin, and the reason that pin is written as an absence. This closes the keyboard row of the acceptance map for the Wall control group; see Visual QA for what is still not driven.\ndocs/archive/plans/2026-09-16-P23.13-architectural-plan-drafting-finish.md:430:- **Fixed — the readout could outlive its focus (§9 readout correctness).** `Escape` cleared the announcement, but two other paths released focus without it: `cancelLocalPlanInteraction` (mode/tool cancel) and `clearLayoutSelection` → `clearPlanFocus`, which is released **inside `layout-interaction`**, where this component cannot hook it. Now `clearPlanKeyboardFocus()` releases ring and readout together at both in-component release points, and the region's text is **derived** from the last keyboard announcement against the live focus (`planAnnouncedFocus.controlId === interaction.planFocus?.id`) with a reconciliation that drops the stored announcement when the focus moves without the keyboard — so no path can leave the region speaking about a control that is no longer focused, including a P23.12-recycled canonical id.\n"}
