Their original attached prototypes were not available through that record.\n\n| Proposed direction | Decision |\n|---|---|\n| Name = meaning; reference = dependable identification; canonical ID = technical identity | **Approve.** Canonical IDs still govern identity internally; references are the human-facing identifier. |\n| Room: required name + reference | **Approve.** Duplicate Room names make references necessary, even when Plan normally shows only names. |\n| Wall/Opening: optional name + reference | **Approve.** Naming every architectural segment must never become housekeeping. |\n| Junction: reference only | **Approve.** |\n| One `O-*` family; Door/Window mutable | **Approve.** |\n| Wall role and shape never affect identity | **Approve.** |\n| Raw IDs behind Technical details; searchable/copyable | **Approve**, with a deliberate exception for explaining raw-ID search matches. |\n| Unnamed Wall/Opening shows reference directly | **Approve.** No placeholder name or missing-name warning. |\n| Navigator protects name/reference; metadata degrades first | **Change:** protect the **complete reference and a useful name excerpt**, not an untruncated name. Keep essential kind/host context readable; remove routine counts and redundant properties first. |\n| Search matches name/reference/raw ID and explains hidden matches | **Change:** also preserve existing kind/role matching. Searching “door” must continue to work after allocator IDs disappear. |\n| Plan shares identity; P23.13 owns placement/density | **Approve.** Sharing identity does not require displaying every tier everywhere. |\n| Duplicate names allowed; references disambiguate | **Approve.** No duplicate-name warning or forced suffixes. |\n| Surviving entities retain references; new entities receive new ones | **Approve.** Survival follows canonical operations, never visual similarity or name matching. |\n| Undo/Redo and Save/Load preserve exact identity | **Approve.** Include import/export round trips and unrelated edits. |\n| No generated semantic fragment names | **Approve.** Also prohibit lineage-looking reference suffixes such as `W-12.1`. |\n\n## 2. Final presentation recommendations\n\n### References\n\n- Family prefixes **R / W / O / J**, hyphen, short uppercase suffix.\n- **Maximum six visible characters**, including prefix and hyphen; for example `W-7K3M`. This specifies a footprint, not an allocation algorithm.\n- Avoid ambiguous characters within suffixes. No encoded order, location, ancestry, kind or geometry.\n- Quiet monospaced text at readable contrast, with **no pill, border or button styling**.\n- Never truncate, wrap or shrink a reference. Budget six character cells at the normal utility-text size.\n- References are unique within the Layout document and non-editable. Do not recycle retired references for different entities.\n\nThe proposals’ opaque-token concern is valid, but fabricated sequence or ancestry is worse. Keep tokens short; names carry memorability.\n\n### Navigator\n\n**Identity line:** name first, complete reference beside it. Unnamed entities show the reference once.\n\n**Optional context line:** relevant kind/relationship, such as `Door · on W-7K3M` or `Also bounds Gallery B`.\n\nDo not repeat `Wall · Boundary` beneath every Wall or force every row into two lines. Reserve that space for information that helps distinguish or understand the entity.\n\nRoom references remain visible in Navigator identity rows. Use the same presentation in search and pinned selection. A shared Wall retains exactly the same identity in every Room context.\n\n### Inspector\n\n- Unified header: **name or reference**, with reference secondary when named; kind stated separately.\n- Room retains **Name**. Wall/Opening gain **Name (optional)**. Clearing an optional name restores reference-led presentation.\n- Full names can wrap in the header and remain editable in the field.\n- **Technical details** starts collapsed, opens by keyboard/touch, and exposes a selectable full canonical ID with **Copy ID**. Preserve disclosure state while inspecting different entities.\n- Provide **Copy reference** in the Inspector, without making Navigator references look actionable.\n- Relationship controls and normal messages use names/references, not allocator strings. Keep existing panel grouping and rename authority.\n\n### Search\n\n- Match name, reference, raw ID, and existing kind/role terms.\n- Highlight visible matching text.\n- When a hidden canonical ID explains the result, add `ID match: …` with the matching portion visible; full ID remains available in Technical details.\n- Preserve Direct / Related / Topology distinctions. Relationship context must explain related results.\n- Exact reference matches must be prominent **within their existing result group**.\n- Selecting a result preserves the query and existing selection/reveal behavior.\n\n### Plan\n\n- Room names remain the normal semantic labels.\n- Existing selected-target feedback uses name/reference instead of internal selection terminology.\n- Wall/Opening/Junction identity is available through selection; P23.12 adds no permanent field of reference labels.\n- Room references support selected-target identification and duplicate-name disambiguation. P23.13 determines their spatial presentation.\n\n### Long names, duplicates and density\n\n- Under pressure: remove redundant metadata → abbreviate relationship summaries → ellipsise name. **Reference survives intact.**\n- Full name remains available through selection in Inspector, not hover alone.\n- Duplicate names retain distinct references; no automatic renaming.\n- If a name exactly equals its own reference, show that string once in compact presentation; Inspector still distinguishes Name from Reference.\n- Validate at **240–300 px**, including nested rows, selection pins and dark/light themes. Do not shrink text to preserve the current row composition.\n\n## 3. Decisions that must be settled\n\nOnly **name inheritance on creation operations** needs an explicit product lock beyond the synthesis. Recommended defaults:\n\n- **Wall split:** surviving Wall keeps its name; genuinely new fragments start unnamed.\n- **Explicit duplicate/repeat:** copy optional Wall/Opening names unchanged, with new references.\n- **Rooms:** preserve existing required-name and reconciliation behavior.\n- **Retired entities:** their references never silently transfer to replacements; Undo restores them.\n\nWith these defaults accepted, no further product-design round is necessary. Reference storage/generation and collision handling belong in implementation planning, subject to the six-character footprint and stability guarantees.\n\n## 4. Final P23.12 design contract\n\n> Every Room, Wall, Opening and Junction has a stable, document-unique compact reference. Rooms retain required names; Walls and Openings support optional names; Junctions remain reference-only.\n>\n> Named entities lead with their name and retain a readable reference. Unnamed entities lead with the reference. Kind and relationships remain separate context. Raw canonical IDs appear in accessible, copyable Technical details and relevant search-match explanations.\n>\n> References use R/W/O/J families, occupy at most six characters, never truncate, and encode no mutable properties or topology lineage. Existing references never change because of renaming, reordering, unrelated edits or property changes. New entities receive new references; retired references are not reassigned. History and persistence restore exact identities.\n>\n> Navigator, Inspector, search, existing Plan identity feedback and architecture-related messages consume one identity vocabulary. Names may truncate in compact surfaces; references may not. Search preserves name/reference/ID and kind/role retrieval.\n>\n> Rename remains Inspector-owned. Split and duplication follow the name-inheritance rules above. No automatic fragment names, inline rename, Junction names, new navigation structure, permanent Plan label system or Inspector restructuring enters P23.12.# P23.12 designer brief — architectural names and stable display identity\n\n**Slice:** P23.12 — Architectural names and stable display identity.\n**Status:** direction only. No implementation-ready child plan exists yet; the\nroadmap is [`docs/plans/2026-09-14-P23-remaining-roadmap-reconciliation.md`](../../plans/2026-09-14-P23-remaining-roadmap-reconciliation.md) §P23.12.\n**Baseline:** [`P23-design-context.md`](./P23-design-context.md) (compiled against\n`main` @ `9118696`, 2026-09-15). **Read that file first** — this brief does not\nrepeat product, workspace or shell context.\n**Purpose:** enumerate the identity-presentation decisions P23.12 needs from a\ndesigner, grounded in the current UI. It does not propose visual design and does\nnot specify implementation.\n\n## Changes since the baseline\n\n**None.** No product or code change has landed between the baseline and this\nbrief. The baseline's Appendix B mismatches all still stand, in particular:\nthe tracker (`docs/plans/README.md`) still says P23.11 is next even though it is\nmerged, and the \"268 px Navigator rail\" figure still exists only in planning docs,\nnot in code (the real rail is `minmax(15rem, --editor-left-width: 300px)` →\n240–300 px).\n\n---\n\n## 1. Current landed state\n\n### 1.1 Room naming **[current]**\n\n| Fact | Where |\n|---|---|\n| `LayoutWallFirstRoom.name: string` is a required, persisted canonical field | `packages/layout-core/src/layout-wall-first-types.ts` |\n| The codec **requires a non-empty name** — a Room with a blank name is invalid | `layout-wall-first-codec.ts` `parseRoom` → `readNonEmptyString(record.name, …)` |\n| Mutation goes through `planRoomMetadataUpdate(document, roomId, { name?, floorThickness?, ceilingThickness? })` | `packages/layout-core/src/layout-wall-topology-ops.ts` |\n| That planner trims the name, rejects empty, **allows duplicate names** (\"`id` is identity\"), rejects unknown keys (the boundary is correspondence-owned), and rejects a no-op so callers write zero history | same file, header comment + `invalid_patch` / `invalid_value` / `no_op` rejections |\n| The Inspector is the only rename surface: selected Room → `<label>Name<input type=\"text\"></label>` calling `updateWallFirstRoomName` | `EditorInspector.svelte` (~2163 / ~1287) |\n| On success the Room survives the edit, so canonical selection and the panel stay put; on rejection the field reverts to the previous value and a status message appears | `updateWallFirstRoomName` |\n| Wall-first Room **context menus expose no Rename item** by explicit policy (\"omit, don't dummy\"); only `Remove room…` | `context-menu/plan-menu-items.ts`; pinned by `tests/lib/layout/p23-6d-room-lifecycle.test.ts` |\n| Auto-created Rooms get a generated authored name `Draft Room 1`, `Draft Room 2`, … (ordinal over *names*, collision-avoiding, never identity) | `layout-room-reconciliation.ts` allocator; `layout-wall-topology-ops.ts:66-71` |\n| Duplicating a Room produces `${source.name} copy`, `${source.name} copy 2`, … | `layout-duplicate.ts:535-540` |\n\n**Consequence for the state matrix:** there is no \"unnamed Room\" state and P23.12\ndoes not create one. Every Room always shows *some* authored name — often a\nmachine-generated one.\n\n### 1.2 Wall / Opening / Junction identity **[current]**\n\n- `LayoutWall`, `LayoutWallOpening` and `LayoutJunction` have **no `name` field**\n  (`layout-wall-first-types.ts`). Their identity is their `id` plus their endpoint\n  Junction IDs (Walls) or host Wall ID (Openings).\n- Canonical IDs are allocator-produced strings such as `wall-chain-1`,\n  `junction-chain-1`, `opening:door:1`, plus split-derived IDs; imported documents\n  may carry arbitrary strings.\n- There is **no** `W044` / `J022` / `D007`-style reference facility anywhere in\n  code. P23.6e Gate 1 recorded it as unavailable and deliberately fell back to\n  formatted IDs.\n\n### 1.3 Where identity text appears today **[current]**\n\n**One shared label derivation.** `formatPlacementLabel(id)`\n(`apps/editor/src/lib/editor/editor-outliner.ts`) trims the ID, splits on\n`-`, `_` and whitespace, and title-cases each part. So `wall-chain-1` renders as\n**`Wall Chain 1`**. It is used by every label path below.\n\n**Caution — the formatter only understands `-`, `_` and whitespace.** Real\nallocator IDs use other separators, and those survive verbatim into the label:\n\n| Real canonical ID | Source | Rendered label today |\n|---|---|---|\n| `wall-chain-1` | Wall chain authoring | `Wall Chain 1` |\n| `wall-chain-1.b`, `wall-chain-1.b.2` | Wall split fragments (`allocateId(…, `${seed}.b`)`) | `Wall Chain 1.b` |\n| `junction-chain-2` | Junction chain authoring | `Junction Chain 2` |\n| `junction-chain-1.2` | Noding/duplicate-derived uniqueness suffix | `Junction Chain 1.2` |\n| `opening:door:1`, `opening:window:3` | `nextWallFirstOpeningId` → `` `opening:${kind}:${index}` `` | `Opening:door:1` |\n| `wall-chain-1:knot:1` | Curve bend point (`layout-wall-centerline.ts`) | `Wall Chain 1:knot:1` |\n\nSo the current primary label for an Opening is literally `Opening:door:1`, and a\nsplit Wall fragment reads `Wall Chain 1.b`. Any reference scheme must be judged\nagainst these inputs, not against a hand-written `W044`.\n\n| Surface | Current treatment | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| Navigator row label (Wall / Opening / Junction / Layout object) | `formatPlacementLabel(id)` — `Wall Chain 1` | `hierarchy-page-projection.ts` (`hierarchyWallRow` / `hierarchyOpeningRow` / `hierarchyJunctionRow` / `hierarchyObjectRow`) |\n| Navigator row label (Room) | authored `name` | `hierarchyRoomRow` |\n| Navigator row **tooltip** | the **raw canonical ID** (`title={row.tooltip ?? row.canonicalId}`) | `hierarchy/HierarchyRow.svelte:114` |\n| Navigator row secondary text (Wall) | `▸ 2 openings` and/or `also in Gallery A, Gallery B, +1`; spelled out only for the `In multiple rooms` filter | `buildWallsRows` |\n| Navigator row secondary text (Opening) | `Door on Wall Chain 1` (host as a formatted ID) | `buildOpeningsRows` |\n| Navigator row secondary text (Junction) | `3 walls` (incident-Wall count) | `hierarchyJunctionRow` |\n| Room page `Ends …` relation row | `` `Ends ${formatPlacementLabel(start)} · ${formatPlacementLabel(end)}` `` — two formatted IDs | `hierarchyEndsRow` (~494) |\n| Pinned selection strip | `Selected {label}` (same derivation) + reason; `title` = **raw entity id**; action `Show in Walls ›` | `HierarchyNavigator.svelte:646-658` |\n| Search — direct match fields | Room: `name`, `roomId`, formatted id · Wall: `wallId`, formatted id, `role` · Opening: `openingId`, formatted id, `openingKind` · Junction: `junctionId`, formatted id · Layout object: `objectId`, formatted id, `objectKind` | `hierarchy-search.ts` |\n| Search block/group headings | `Rooms / Walls / Openings / Junctions / Layout Objects / Scene Content`, then `Direct matches / Related / Topology` | `hierarchy-search.ts` |\n| Plan | Room name only (`room-name` style, drawn on the room face with legibility floors). Selected Opening adds a top-left `door · 0.90 m × 2.10 m` selection label. Dimension readouts show meters, never IDs. **No Wall / Junction / Room / Opening ID is drawn on Plan.** | `layout/plan-overlays.ts`; `layout/LayoutPlanViewport.svelte:3520` |\n| Inspector header line | `Layout Plan editing · preview-only` (domain context, not identity) | `EditorInspector.svelte` header |\n| Inspector selected entity — **Room** | `<strong>{name}</strong>` + `<span>{id}</span>` (raw id on its own line) + `Boundary walls: wall-chain-1, …` (raw Wall ID list); names appear for `Bounded rooms` only | ~2158 |\n| Inspector selected entity — **Wall** | `<strong>Wall {id}</strong>` → e.g. `Wall wall-chain-1`; also `start → end · 4.20 m`; endpoint buttons labelled with raw Junction IDs; hosted-Opening buttons labelled `door · opening:door:1`; `Bounded rooms` shows Room **names** | ~1993–2050 |\n| Inspector selected entity — **Junction** | `<strong>Junction {id}</strong>` + `Connected Wall geometry follows this Junction.` + X (m) / Z (m) | ~2060 |\n| Inspector selected entity — **Opening** | `<strong>{kind} opening</strong>` + `Opening: {id}` + `Wall: {id} · 4.20 m · boundary` | ~2075 |\n| Inspector selected entity — **Layout object** | `<strong>{kind} object</strong>` + `<span>{id}</span>` | ~1915 |\n| Inspector Align reference dropdown | option labels `{kind} · {raw id}` and `Room bounds · {raw room id}` | `alignObjectOptions` / `alignRoomOptions` |\n| Inspector Objects accordion rows | `{object.kind}` + raw `{object.id}` | ~1870 |\n| Inspector Topology diagnostics rows | `{issue.code}` + `{targetId} · {issue.message}` | ~1889 |\n| Mutation status messages | include raw IDs, e.g. `Added a bend point to Wall wall-chain-1`, `Room rejected: Room name cannot be empty` | `EditorInspector.svelte`, `layout-preview-state.svelte.ts` |\n| Outliner search / camera surfaces | not P23.12 scope (Camera has its own inspector labels) | — |\n\n**So today the same entity is named three different ways in one screen:**\n`Wall Chain 1` in the rail (plus `wall-chain-1` in its tooltip), `Wall wall-chain-1`\nin the Inspector, and nothing at all on the canvas.\n\n### 1.4 Current Navigator density and legibility **[current]**\n\nConfirmed density/legibility problems in the real rail (§2 of the baseline\n## 2. Fixed P23.12 requirements\n\nThese are settled by the roadmap. They are inputs to design, not questions.\n\n1. **Authored Room name remains the existing authority** — same field, same\n   mutation path, same Inspector surface. P23.12 must not add a second Room naming\n   field or path.\n2. **Walls and Openings gain optional authored names** as Layout metadata, written\n   through one canonical metadata planner/adapter/transaction, edited in the\n   selected entity's Inspector panel.\n3. **Junctions remain reference-only** — no authored Junction names.\n4. **Walls, Openings and Junctions each get a stable compact reference**, and the\n   one display-identity contract is consumed by Plan, Navigator, search and\n   Inspector. **Whether Rooms also receive a compact reference is not stated in the\n   roadmap** — Rooms already have an authored name, and the exit criterion for\n   Rooms is only \"a concise, accessible, unambiguous label everywhere it appears\".\n   Treat \"does a Room need a reference too?\" as an open question for the child\n   plan, not as a settled requirement.\n5. **Authored name is primary where one exists.**\n6. **The compact reference remains available as the unambiguous secondary\n   identity** where a name is primary.\n7. **Unnamed entities use the compact reference as the primary label.**\n8. **Raw canonical IDs remain available as secondary / debug detail** — never the\n   only label, never removed as an escape hatch.\n9. **Search indexes authored names, compact references and raw canonical IDs.**\n10. **Rename stays Inspector-owned.** No inline Navigator rename, no context-menu\n    rename, no second mutation surface.\n11. **Display identity never replaces canonical identity or connectivity.** Names\n    and references are presentation; canonical IDs remain the identity and\n    connectivity authority, and references must never depend on document order.\n12. **Stability:** references survive reorder, deletion, insertion, split,\n    Save/Load and Undo/Redo; renaming never changes topology, selection identity or\n    Scene/Camera; duplicate names are legal and must not break identity.\n\n**Not yet decided and therefore an open question, not a requirement to satisfy:**\nthe *mechanism* of the compact reference. The roadmap requires the future child\nplan to ratify either persisted presentation metadata or a deterministic\ncollision-safe derivative of the canonical ID, and explicitly forbids\ndocument-order ordinals. A designer should treat the *shape* of a reference as\nunknown (not necessarily `W044`) and design for a short, stable, non-sequential\ntoken.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Design decisions needed\n\nNine decisions. For each: what is being decided, the concrete current UI state it\naffects, and what is already fixed. **No answer is proposed here.**\n\n### D1 — Named vs unnamed presentation\n\n- **Decide:** how a *named* Wall/Opening reads versus an *unnamed* one, such that\n  the two states are distinguishable without one looking like an error state, and\n  such that a Room's machine-generated `Draft Room 2` name reads as a real name\n  rather than a placeholder (it is a real authored name — the codec rejects empty).\n- **Current state:** unnamed Wall rows show `Wall Chain 1` in the rail and\n  `Wall wall-chain-1` in the Inspector; Room rows show `Draft Room 2`.\n  Nothing marks whether those strings are authored or generated.\n- **Affected:** every Navigator row (`HierarchyRow.svelte`), every Inspector\n  selected-entity block (`EditorInspector.svelte` ~1915–2163), Plan Room labels\n  (`plan-overlays.ts` `room-name`).\n- **Fixed:** unnamed entities must use the compact reference as the primary label;\n  named entities must lead with the name; raw IDs stay reachable.\n\n### D2 — Name + reference hierarchy\n\n- **Decide:** the reading order and visual weight of name → reference → raw ID in a\n  one-line context (rail row, Inspector header). Which of the three is visible by\n  default, which is revealed on demand, and how the secondary tier is visually\n  subordinated without becoming illegible.\n- **Current state:** the rail shows one label + one right-aligned meta; the\n  Inspector shows a bold header plus one or more plain `<span>` lines. Raw ID\n  visibility differs per surface today (§1.3), which makes the tiering inconsistent\n  before P23.12 even starts.\n- **Affected:** `HierarchyRow.svelte` `.tree-row__label` / `.tree-row__meta`;\n  Inspector `layout-selected-room` block header.\n- **Fixed:** all three tiers must exist and be retrievable; the name is primary\n  where present; the raw ID is secondary/debug, not primary.\n\n### D3 — Navigator row treatment\n\n| Pinned policies (tests) | `apps/editor/tests/lib/layout/p23-6d-room-lifecycle.test.ts`; `apps/editor/tests/lib/editor/app/p23-6e-hierarchy-projection.test.ts` |\n\n### 6.2 Mismatches between current code and the roadmap\n\n1. **The `Ends` row is more prominent than the roadmap implies.** The roadmap\n   describes it as one row inside an expanded Room Wall disclosure; that is\n   accurate, but its label is a *single sentence-length line built from two\n   formatted IDs* (`Ends Junction Chain 1 · Junction Chain 2`) inside an already\n   indented child block. Screenshots should show it at the real rail width.\n2. **Search can match an ID it does not display.** The roadmap lists raw-ID search\n   as a P23.12 addition to preserve; raw-ID search **already exists**\n   (`hierarchy-search.ts` matches `wallId`/`openingId`/`junctionId`), and today it\n   produces results whose visible label does not explain the match. P23.12's job is\n   therefore partly to make an existing capability legible, not only to add one.\n3. **\"Unnamed\" is not a supported Room state.** The roadmap's presentation contract\n   speaks of \"unnamed architecture\"; for Rooms the codec requires a non-empty name\n   and auto-generates `Draft Room N`. Only Walls/Openings/Junctions can be unnamed.\n4. **The rail width figure is unverified.** Roadmap and P23.6e directive say\n   268 px; code has 240–300 px. Confirm the intended width before judging density.\n5. **The Inspector already exposes raw IDs in more places than \"debug detail\"\n   suggests** (Boundary walls list, endpoint/hosted-Opening buttons, Align dropdown,\n   Objects accordion, diagnostics rows). Any tiering decision has to cover these or\n   explicitly leave them.\n6. **The current fallback label is worse than \"formatted raw ID\" implies.** The\n   roadmap's shorthand (`W044`, `J022`, `D007`) reads like a tidy token; the shipped\n   formatter only splits on `-`, `_` and whitespace, so real allocator IDs surface\n   as `Opening:door:1`, `Wall Chain 1.b` and `Junction Chain 1.2` (§1.3 table).\n   P23.12 is not just adding a reference — it is replacing a fallback that is\n   currently illegible for a whole entity family.\n\n### 6.3 Assumptions screenshots should confirm\n\n- The identity text actually rendered for a Wall/Opening/Junction row at the real\n  rail width, including how much of the label and meta survive truncation.\n- Whether the `Ends` row wraps, clips or ellipsises at that width, and how deep the\n  child indentation makes it.\n- The exact Inspector header stack for each of the five entity kinds, in one\n  screenshot set, so the four-pattern inconsistency (§1.3) is visible rather than\n  asserted.\n- What a real project's IDs look like (allocator strings such as `wall-chain-1`,\n  `junction-chain-2`, `opening:door:1` after a few splits) versus an imported\n  document's arbitrary IDs — the reference design must survive both.\n- Whether any authored name in a working project already collides with a formatted\n  ID string (e.g. `Draft Room 1` next to a reference that reads similarly).\n- Dark theme (default navy) and at least one contrasting theme (e.g.\n  `porcelain-atelier`) for the secondary-tier contrast check.\n\n### 6.4 Most useful UI states to capture\n## Display identity (P23.12, landed)\n\nEvery Room, Wall, Opening and Junction has a stable, document-unique compact\nreference. Rooms retain required names; Walls and Openings support optional\nnames; Junctions remain reference-only.\n\n- Named entities lead with their name and retain a readable reference. Unnamed\n  entities lead with the reference. Kind and relationships remain separate\n  context. Raw canonical IDs appear in accessible, copyable Technical details\n  and relevant search-match explanations.\n- References use R/W/O/J families, occupy at most six characters, never\n  truncate, and encode no mutable properties or topology lineage. Existing\n  references never change because of renaming, reordering, unrelated edits or\n  property changes. New entities receive new references; retired references are\n  not reassigned. History and persistence restore exact identities.\n- Navigator, Inspector, search, existing Plan identity feedback and\n  architecture-related messages consume one identity vocabulary. Names may\n  truncate in compact surfaces; references may not. Search preserves\n  name/reference/ID and kind/role retrieval. Rename remains Inspector-owned.\n- No generated semantic fragment names; no lineage-looking suffixes such as\n  `W-12.1`. No duplicate-name warnings or forced suffixes — references\n  disambiguate.\n\nRationale, presentation detail and acceptance record:\n`docs/archive/roadmap/p23/p23.12-final-design-contract.md`.\n\n## Plan keyboard invariants (P23.13, landed)\n\n- **Arrows never enter the control group** (A5: Enter enters, arrows traverse).\n  `planTraversalStep` returns `null` outside the group; `planTraversalEnteredFor`\n  requires keyboard entry — pointer focus also sets `planFocus`, so entry inferred\n  from focus let clicks steal arrows/scrolling. Do not revert to roving-tabindex.\n- **The keyboard readout is a keyboard instrument**: retired by Escape, mode/tool\n  cancel, canvas-reaching primary press, or successful exact edit — never by\n  pointer focus alone. Clear it, do not recompute per change (§9: one announcement\n  per meaningful change). Known residue: Undo moving the focused control leaves\n"}


### 8. Final report

Timestamp: `2026-09-18T14:36:28.608Z`.

```markdown
P23.12 separated **meaning (authored name), dependable human identification (reference), and technical identity (canonical ID)**.

The historical brief documented poor existing labels: `Opening:door:1`, `Wall Chain 1.b`, and inconsistent names for the same entity across Navigator and Inspector. Imported IDs could be arbitrary. Compact references solved that presentation problem while keeping canonical IDs authoritative internally and accessible through Technical details/search.

The final design chose:

- **R/W/O/J, maximum six characters:** readable in the real 240–300 px rail, never truncated.
- **Stable, non-semantic references:** no mutable Door/Window kind, wall shape, order, location, or split ancestry encoded. The contract explicitly judged fabricated sequence/ancestry worse than short opaque tokens; names supply memorability.
- **Room references too:** duplicate Room names require disambiguation.
- **Optional Wall/Opening names:** naming every segment must not become housekeeping; unnamed entities use references directly.
- **Inspector-owned rename:** preserve the existing Room naming authority and one canonical mutation path; extend it to Wall/Opening metadata without adding inline/context-menu rename or restructuring navigation.
- **One vocabulary across surfaces:** Navigator, Inspector, search, and existing Plan feedback agree without filling the Plan with permanent labels.

Sources: [historical brief](/Users/tony/Documents/Biskiq/spatial-sketch-editor/docs/archive/roadmap/p23/p23.12-designer-brief.md), [final design contract](/Users/tony/Documents/Biskiq/spatial-sketch-editor/docs/archive/roadmap/p23/p23.12-final-design-contract.md), [landed shell contract](/Users/tony/Documents/Biskiq/spatial-sketch-editor/docs/reference/components/shell.md:125).

Probe record:

- **FILES OPENED:** `docs/README.md` full; final design contract full; designer brief snippets 1–110, 170–250, 458–505; shell contract snippet 125–160. All paths rooted at `/Users/tony/Documents/Biskiq/spatial-sketch-editor/`.
- **Search:** exact P23.12/reference terms across archive, shell contract, roadmap router; returned incidental matches from legacy tracker, P23.13 brief/research/plans/proposals.
- **SKILLS LOADED:** none; historical explanation required no workflow skill.
- **WHY:** router permits archive for explicitly requested historical rationale; historical brief supplies the problem and existing ownership, final contract supplies decisions.
- **UNNECESSARY READS:** broad archive search produced unrelated P23.13 matches and truncation. Could have read shell identity section first, then followed its precise archive pointer. Last few shell lines concern unrelated keyboard invariants.
- **AUTHORITY SELECTED:** historical final contract for rationale; brief for contemporary problem statement; current shell contract corroborates landed behavior.
- **STOP POINT:** those documents fully answered question; no source-code or further history exploration needed.
- **UNNECESSARY QUESTION/PERMISSION:** none.
- **RESULT:** answered read-only; no changes.
- **APPROXIMATE BYTES:** ~51 KB tool-returned text, including incidental search snippets.
```
